# Transcripts: `haiku/raw-files`

Generated by render.py from raw/*.jsonl and *.meta.json; nothing edited.

45 answers, in question order. The evidence is `raw/<qid>-r<n>.jsonl` (every event, untouched) and `raw/<qid>-r<n>.meta.json` (prompt, system prompt, settings, exact command). Tool calls are cut at 400 characters and results at 500; the final answer is whole. The one rewrite: the home directory of the machine that ran it appears as `<HOME>`.

## Q1-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 62.5 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Frequency: across all five years, what percentage of cases have base section 16(h), and what percentage have base section 20(a)(2)? Round to one decimal.
```

**System prompt:**

```
You are a careful analyst. You can use the Read, Grep, Glob and Bash tools (Bash has Python 3). Your working directory is the only place you may look for files; use only files in it. Show the number or answer each question asks for in your final reply.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (all 58 orders, all years pooled):**

- **Base section 16(h): 39.6%** (3,939 of 9,958 cases)
- **Base section 20(a)(2): 22.9%** (2,284 of 9,958 cases)

**Method**

- I used the text extracts in `data/text/`, one page per recommendation form. The cover page of each order was excluded.
- I took the citation under "finds the following violation(s)" and stripped the "IC 8-1-26-" prefix and any parenthesized word of two or more letters. Single-character parentheticals such as (h) and (a)(2) were kept.
- OCR errors were handled. "8-l-26" and "(l)" were read as "8-1-26" and "(1)". The one "lS(h)" citation (PSDNPT_040622, case 24633) was read as 18(h). Neither change affects the two numerators.

**Denominator adjustments**

- I excluded 5 blank template pages with no case number: 030321 p.140, 040622 p.275, 071421 pp.30, 79 and 175. That leaves 9,958 cases.
- Case 24409 (ord_PSD_022322, p.129) has a case number but no citation in the text layer. I kept it in the denominator and did not count it as either base section. I could not render the PDF to read it, since `pdftoppm` is not installed. Its effect on the percentages is at most about 0.01 points.
```

## Q1-r2

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 35.6 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Frequency: across all five years, what percentage of cases have base section 16(h), and what percentage have base section 20(a)(2)? Round to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer**

- **Base section 16(h):** 3,939 of 9,957 cases = **39.6%**
- **Base section 20(a)(2):** 2,284 of 9,957 cases = **22.9%**

**Method and caveats**

- I used the OCR text files for all 58 orders (2021–2025). The cover pages were excluded, leaving 9,963 case forms.
- Six case forms have no readable cite in the text (no IC 8-1-26 citation printed after "finds the following violation(s)"). I could not check them against the PDFs because no PDF rendering tool is installed. Those six are excluded from the denominator. If they are counted in the denominator instead, the shares are 39.5% and 22.9%.
- Parenthesized words were stripped as the definitions require. A few OCR errors such as "20(a)(l)" (7 cases) and "lS(h)" (1 case) were left as printed. They do not change either target count.
```

```
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.

  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are

- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but

  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Base section 16(h): 39.5%** (3,935 of 9,951 cases)
**Base section 20(a)(2): 22.9%** (2,283 of 9,951 cases)

The denominator is 9,951 cases, each with one citation. Both percentages are unchanged, at one decimal, if the one unreadable case below is counted in the denominator (9,952).

Data handling:
- **Denominator:** I counted 9,957 "finds the following violation(s)" headings. Six had no citation text nearby. Five of those are blank form templates (no respondent, case number or damage data), so I excluded them. The sixth is a real case (case 24409, Cobalt Civil, order ord_PSD_022322, page 129), but its citation is absent from the text layer. I could not render the PDF page (pdftoppm is not installed), so I could not read it. It is the only unverified case.
- **OCR repairs:** some citations were misread, e.g. "8-l-26" for "8-1-26", "(l)" for "(1)", and "lS(h)" for "18(h)". I corrected these. The "lS(h)" case is an inference from context.
- **Base sections:** I stripped the "IC 8-1-26-" prefix and any parenthesized word of two or more letters. Single-character parentheses such as (h) and (a)(2) were kept. The 16(h) count includes (expire), (prior), (out) and (other) variants. The 20(a)(2) count includes one "(a)(2)(expire)" citation.
```

## Q2-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 27.3 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Trend: how many cases are there in each year 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024 and 2025, and which year has the most?
```

```
You are a careful analyst. You can use the Read, Grep, Glob and Bash tools (Bash has Python 3). Your working directory is the only place you may look for files; use only files in it. Show the number or answer each question asks for in your final reply.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Trend: how many cases are there in each year 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024 and 2025, and which year has the most?

```
```
**Cases per order year (2021–2025):**

| Year | Orders (files) | Cases |
|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 11 | **1,603** |
| 2022 | 12 | **2,672** |
| 2023 | 14 | **2,107** |
```

```
**2022 has the most cases.** The total across all 58 orders is 9,963.

**How I got these numbers:**
- I counted one case per recommendation form, using the "Recommendation to IURC" header. Each count matched the "Respondent:" line in its file.
- The order's printed date is not in the extracted text. The only dates in the text are advisory-meeting and damage dates, which don't match the order date. I took the order year from the MMDDYY (or MM.DD.YY) suffix of each file name. This is an inference, but the suffixes are consistent with the advisory-meeting dates. If the printed order dates differ, the year assignments could change.
- `ord_PSDNPT_040622` is a nunc pro tunc correction of case 24633, which also appears in `ord_PSD_022322`. I counted it as one case in 2022, since it is one form in its own order. Excluding it would give 2022 = 2,671 and a total of 9,962. The 2022 lead is unchanged either way.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Cases per year (by order date):**

| Year | Orders | Cases |
|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 11 | 1,603 |
| 2022 | 12 | 2,672 |
| 2023 | 14 | 2,107 |
| 2024 | 11 | 1,897 |
| 2025 | 10 | 1,684 |
| **Total** | **58** | **9,963** |

**2022 has the most cases**, with 2,672.

How I got these numbers:
- **Case count:** I counted the "Case Number:" entries in each order's text. Each recommendation form is one page, and in every order the count matches the number of "Recommendation to IURC" forms. The first page of each order is the Commission's cover order, so it is not a case.
- **Order year:** The order text does not print its own date. I used the year in the filename, which matches the PDF creation-date metadata for all 57 orders that have it. The one exception is `ord_PSD_09.04.24`, which has no metadata, so its 2024 year comes from the filename alone.
```

_11 tool calls; list price $0.0079; stop reason end_turn_

## Q3-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 98.0 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Severity by violation: among cases with a headline penalty above $0, what is the mean headline penalty for base section 16(h), and for base section 20(a)(2)? Round each to the nearest dollar.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (headline penalty > $0 only):**

- **Base section 16(h): $4,199** (mean of 2,501 cases; total $10,500,900; unrounded 4,198.68)
- **Base section 20(a)(2): $3,787** (mean of 1,888 cases; total $7,150,700; unrounded 3,787.45)

**How I got them**

- I parsed the 58 text extracts in `data/text/`, which contain 10,021 pages. I kept the 9,957 recommendation forms that carry an IC 8-1-26 cite. Blank templates and one "Trained" follow-up page with no cite were excluded.
- OCR errors were normalized before reading cites. Examples: "8-l-26" became "8-1-26", "(l)" became "(1)", and "lS(h)" in the nunc pro tunc order was read as 18(h), the same case 24633 as the original.
- Headline penalty is the first printed "$" amount after "recommends", skipping "$" blanks and applying the doubled-dollar and thousands-space rules. Forms with no "$" amount count as $0 and are excluded from these means, as the definitions require.

**Caveats**

- Two 20(a)(2) forms print a bare amount with no "$": ".5000" (ord_PSD_020321, p. 27) and "3000" (ord_PSD_060822, p. 290). Under the definition they are $0. If they were read as $5,000 and $3,000, the 20(a)(2) mean would be $3,790.
- Case 33669 appears on two identical pages in one order (pp. 245–246). I counted both, per the one-form-per-page definition. Counting it once gives $3,787 as well, so the answer doesn't change.
- Case 25064 appears twice in one order under different cites (16(h) and 20(a)(2)). Both count under their own base sections.
```

## Q3-r2

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 50.1 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Severity by violation: among cases with a headline penalty above $0, what is the mean headline penalty for base section 16(h), and for base section 20(a)(2)? Round each to the nearest dollar.
```


**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Mean headline penalty, among cases with a headline penalty above $0:**

- **Base section 16(h): $4,192** (exact mean $4,191.997; 2,424 cases)
- **Base section 20(a)(2): $3,795** (exact mean $3,795.391; 1,866 cases)

Method: I parsed the text layer of all 58 order PDFs and counted only recommendation pages that carry a case number. I dropped 186 blank template pages, which left 9,777 cases. I read cites with OCR variants normalized (for example "8-l-26", "(l)", "lS(h)"), so "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" counts as 16(h). The headline penalty is the first dollar amount in the recommendation, with doubled "$" and thousands-separator spaces handled as specified.

The cases I excluded for the means were those with a $0 headline: 1,383 cases for 16(h) and 387 cases for 20(a)(2). These are mostly warning letters and training-only outcomes.
```

## Q3-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 60.9 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Severity by violation: among cases with a headline penalty above $0, what is the mean headline penalty for base section 16(h), and for base section 20(a)(2)? Round each to the nearest dollar.
```


**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (headline penalty > $0 only):**

- **Base section 16(h): $4,199** (mean of $10,500,900 over 2,501 cases, unrounded $4,198.68)
- **Base section 20(a)(2): $3,787** (mean of $7,150,700 over 1,888 cases, unrounded $3,787.45)

**Method:** I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text/`, splitting each order into recommendation forms (9,963 in total). For each form I took the cite after "finds the following violation(s)" and reduced it to its base section by dropping parenthesized words of two or more letters. I took the headline penalty as the first dollar amount in the recommendation, after collapsing "$$" and spaces after thousands commas. Forms with no dollar amount count as $0 and are excluded from the means.

**Caveats:**

1. **One OCR-garbled penalty.** Form 20(a)(2) on page 27 of `ord_PSD_020321` reads ".5000", which I did not count. If it is $5,000, the 20(a)(2) mean becomes $3,788. I could not check it against the PDF because the PDF renderer (pdftoppm) is not installed in this environment.
2. **Seven forms have no readable cite in the text layer,** so I could not assign them to a base section. Two of them carry a headline penalty: the form on page 129 of `ord_PSD_022322` ($5,000 training alternative) and the form on page 2 of `ord_PSDNPT_040622` ($1,000). Their base sections are unknown, so they could change either mean.
3. **Checkbox forms.** Some forms (e.g., page 149 of `ord_PSD_042121`) list several penalty options as checkboxes. I used the first dollar amount printed, which is the rule as stated, but the amount actually selected may differ.
```

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Warnings: what percentage of utility-respondent cases, and what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases, have a recommendation containing the words "warning letter" (any capitalization)? Round to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers**

- **Utility-respondent cases:** 1 of 2,714 has "warning letter" in its recommendation, so **0.0%**.
- **Non-utility-respondent cases:** 2,039 of 7,249 do, so **28.1%**.

**Method.** I parsed the 58 extracted order texts in `data/text/`, which contain 9,963 recommendation forms. For each form I took the respondent name and the text between "recommends the following … penalty:" and the next "Abstentions:", "Additional Information", or "APPROVED:" marker. I then matched "warning letter" in that text, ignoring case and any whitespace between the two words.

**Caveats:**
- **Respondent names:** 187 forms use a different checkbox layout where the respondent name sits on its own line below the field labels. I read the name from that position. 39 of those forms have no respondent name at all, so they count as non-utility. The matching also ignores spaces, to handle OCR splits such as "N orthem".
- **Checkbox template:** 7 forms show "D Warning letter" as a checkbox-style option. I counted them because the literal words are present. If those boxes were unchecked, the non-utility figure would drop slightly.
- **Utility count:** the single utility-respondent match is ord_PSD_113022, respondent "Citizens Energy Group – Steam".
- **Extraction:** I worked from the text extraction, not the original PDFs.
```

```
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers**

- **Utility respondents:** **0.0%** (1 of 2,713 cases). The one match is Citizens Energy Group – Steam Division (case 27398, order ord_PSD_113022, cite IC 8-1-26-20(a)(2)).
- **Non-utility respondents:** **27.8%** (1,966 of 7,064 cases).

**Method**

- I split each order text file by page and treated each page with a case number as one case, giving 9,777 cases across the 58 orders. I skipped 186 blank form pages that have no case number.
- "Recommendation" means the text after "recommends the following …:" up to "Abstentions:" or "APPROVED". Additional Information was not searched.
- "Warning letter" was matched case-insensitively.

**Sensitivity**

- Two cases have no respondent name, so I classed them as non-utility. Case 20352 is ambiguous because "Vectren Energy Delivery" appears in the Other Party position of the layout. Classing it as utility would give 0.0% (1/2,714) for utilities and still 27.8% for non-utilities, so the answers do not change.
- Case 20352 also has a garbled checkbox entry ("D W aming letter") that does not contain the words "warning letter" as extracted. I did not count it. It is the only non-match I would revisit by looking at the scanned PDF.
- Two pairs of cases appear on two pages each (25064 in ord_PSD_051822 and 33669 in ord_PSD_101525). I counted each page as a separate case, following the "one case number on one page" definition.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Warnings: what percentage of utility-respondent cases, and what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases, have a recommendation containing the words "warning letter" (any capitalization)? Round to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Utility-respondent cases: 0.0%** (1 of 2,718 cases contain "warning letter")
**Non-utility-respondent cases: 28.1%** (2,039 of 7,244 cases contain "warning letter")

Method: I parsed all 58 order texts in `data/text/` and split them into recommendation forms, giving 9,962 cases after dropping one blank template page. Each case's respondent was classified with the prefix list from the definitions, and "warning letter" was matched case-insensitively with whitespace normalised. The one utility case with a match is Citizens Energy Group - Steam Division (ord_PSD_113022, p.175).

Two judgment calls affect the non-utility figure:
- Seven checkbox-style forms (ord_PSD_042121, pp.149–156) list "Warning letter" as a printed option in every form. I counted them as containing the words, as the literal definition requires. Six of them are the only checkbox-layout forms with a match. The seventh (p.155) reads "W aming letter", a likely OCR error, so it is not counted in the 28.1%. Counting it would give 2,040 / 7,244 = 28.2%.
- The utility figure is not affected by either issue.
```

## Q5-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 70.4 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Training option: among cases with a headline penalty above $0, what percentage have a recommendation that contains the word "training" (any capitalization)? Round to one decimal.
```

```
You are a careful analyst. You can use the Read, Grep, Glob and Bash tools (Bash has Python 3). Your working directory is the only place you may look for files; use only files in it. Show the number or answer each question asks for in your final reply.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 44.7%** (3,532 of 7,899 cases with a headline penalty above $0 have "training" in the recommendation).

Method:
- I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text/` and found 9,963 recommendation forms (one per case).
- 2,064 cases have a headline penalty of $0, mostly "Warning letter" with no dollar amount, plus a few blank or "no penalty recommended" forms. These are excluded from the base.
- Headline penalties use the stated rules, including "$$1000" and "$3, 500" (read as $3,500).
- Of the 7,899 cases with a penalty above $0, 3,532 contain "training" (any capitalization), which is 44.715%, rounded to 44.7%.

Caveats:
- The source is OCR-style text extracted from the PDFs, so I checked the form counts against the page counts and handled the layout variants I found (a label-first layout in one order, and a "penalty:" wording without "civil" in another).
- Eight checkbox-style forms in the 2021 order ORD_PSD_042121 list every option, including unchecked ones. I read the headline penalty literally as the first "$" printed. Those eight all contain "training," and excluding them gives 3,524 of 7,891, which still rounds to 44.7%.
```

## Q5-r2

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 99.5 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Training option: among cases with a headline penalty above $0, what percentage have a recommendation that contains the word "training" (any capitalization)? Round to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Training option: 44.7%** (3,532 of 7,899 cases with a headline penalty above $0).

Method: I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text`, one recommendation form per page. The headline penalty is the first "$" amount in the recommendation, and "training" is matched case-insensitively within that recommendation text only.

Base counts:
- 9,963 recommendation forms in the text files.
- 5 blank template pages excluded (030321 p.140, 040622 p.275, 071421 pp.30, 79, 175). They have no respondent, case, cite or recommendation in the text layer. I could not render the PDFs to confirm them visually.
- 9,958 forms remain. 2,059 have a headline penalty of $0 (mostly "Warning letter"), leaving 7,899 with a headline above $0.
- 3,532 of those 7,899 contain "training".

Judgement calls that affect the result:
- **8 checkbox forms** (042121 pp.149–156) were read from the checkbox marks in the text layer, since I could not render the PDF. All 8 select a recommendation containing "training" (seven at $5,000, one at $1,500), so each is counted as a training case with a positive headline. If any mark was misread, the percentage would shift.
- **Arc American, 022322 p.196** has no recommendation text, so I treated its headline as $0 and excluded it.
- **".5000" (020321 p.27)** has no "$" sign, so by the rule its headline is $0 and it is excluded.
```

## Q5-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 84.5 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Training option: among cases with a headline penalty above $0, what percentage have a recommendation that contains the word "training" (any capitalization)? Round to one decimal.
```

```
You are a careful analyst. You can use the Read, Grep, Glob and Bash tools (Bash has Python 3). Your working directory is the only place you may look for files; use only files in it. Show the number or answer each question asks for in your final reply.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 44.7%** (3,532 of 7,899 cases with a headline penalty above $0 have "training" in the recommendation).

How I got there, from the order text files in `data/text/`:

- **Cases:** 9,958 recommendation forms across the 58 orders. I excluded 5 blank template pages with no respondent, case, cite, or recommendation text.
- **Headline penalty:** I took the first dollar amount in each recommendation, after collapsing "$$" to "$" and removing spaces after thousands commas. 7,899 cases have a headline above $0; the other cases have no dollar amount.
- **Training:** I counted a recommendation as containing "training" if it matches the word case-insensitively. "Train", "trained", and "can train" do not count, since the question asks for the word "training." This gives 3,532 matches.

Caveats:
- The 7 checkbox-style forms (all in the 2021 order ord_PSD_042121) list every option whether or not it is checked. Every one of them has a headline above $0 and contains "training" under either reading, so the answer does not change.
- Some forms in ord_PSD_110321 lack a printed case number, but I still counted them as cases because they have a recommendation and cite.
- I worked from the extracted text layer, which has OCR errors. I did not check against the PDF images, so a few figures could be affected by misreads.
```

## Q6-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 85.2 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Repeat concentration: take non-utility-respondent cases only. Normalize each respondent name, in this order: lowercase; delete every character that is not a letter, a digit or a space (no replacement); collapse runs of spaces to one and trim; then, once, drop a final whole word if it is exactly "inc", "llc", "co", "corp", "corporation" or "company". How many distinct normalized respondents have 5 or more cases, and what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases do they account for? Round the percentage to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 187 distinct normalized non-utility respondents have 5 or more cases. Together they account for 1,760 of 7,242 non-utility-respondent cases, or 24.3%.**

How I got there:

- **Source:** I used the OCR text in `data/text/` (58 orders). The per-order PDFs were not needed.
- **Cases:** 9,957 recommendation forms. I excluded one cover page (`ord_PSDNPT_040622` p.1) and five blank template pages that have no case number or cite.
- **Split:** 2,715 utility-respondent cases and 7,242 non-utility-respondent cases.
- **Normalization:** applied in the order you specified. The denominator is 7,242 and the percentage is 24.302…%, rounded to 24.3%.
- **Distinct non-utility respondents:** 4,725 before the 5-case threshold.

Caveats:

- **OCR noise:** the text has OCR errors, such as "internattional" and "milenium". A true repeat respondent could be split across two spellings and fall below the threshold, so the count could be slightly understated.
- **Blank respondent:** one case (`ord_PSD_020823` p.190, case 28073) has a blank respondent field. I counted it as non-utility in the denominator. It does not affect the group count.
- **Uncertain legacy page:** `ord_PSD_110321` p.121 is garbled. I read its respondent as "Shipshewana", which is a single group member and does not change the result.
```

## Q6-r2

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 104.3 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Repeat concentration: take non-utility-respondent cases only. Normalize each respondent name, in this order: lowercase; delete every character that is not a letter, a digit or a space (no replacement); collapse runs of spaces to one and trim; then, once, drop a final whole word if it is exactly "inc", "llc", "co", "corp", "corporation" or "company". How many distinct normalized respondents have 5 or more cases, and what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases do they account for? Round the percentage to one decimal.
```

```
**Answer: 187 distinct normalized respondents have 5 or more cases. Together they account for 1,760 of 7,243 non-utility-respondent cases, or 24.3%.**
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 187 distinct normalized respondents have 5 or more cases. Together they account for 1,760 of 7,243 non-utility-respondent cases, or 24.3%.**

How I got there:

- **Source:** I parsed the 58 OCR text files in `data/text/` (not the PDFs), one case per recommendation page. That gives 9,958 cases.
- **Utility split:** 2,715 utility-respondent cases and 7,243 non-utility-respondent cases.
- **Normalization:** I applied the rules literally, in the stated order. OCR noise survives normalization, so "Internattional Inc." (16 cases) and "Milenium" (36) are counted as they appear.
- **Parsing:** Two layouts were handled. In the standard form, respondent names wrapped onto a continuation line were joined. In the alternate form, values sit below the labels and were read in label order. Blank template pages were excluded.
- **Edge cases:** One case (ord_PSD_020823, p.190) has a blank respondent field. It has an empty normalized name, so it cannot be in a group of 5 or more, but it stays in the denominator. Excluding it would give 24.31%, which still rounds to 24.3%.
```

## Q6-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 82.8 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Repeat concentration: take non-utility-respondent cases only. Normalize each respondent name, in this order: lowercase; delete every character that is not a letter, a digit or a space (no replacement); collapse runs of spaces to one and trim; then, once, drop a final whole word if it is exactly "inc", "llc", "co", "corp", "corporation" or "company". How many distinct normalized respondents have 5 or more cases, and what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases do they account for? Round the percentage to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 187 distinct normalized respondents have 5 or more non-utility cases. Together they account for 1,759 of 7,243 non-utility-respondent cases, or 24.3%.**

How I got there:

- **Source:** I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text/`, splitting them by page. Each order has one cover page, and the rest are recommendation forms.
- **Case count:** 9,958 cases in total. That is the 10,021 pages less the 58 covers and 5 blank template pages with no respondent, case number, or citation.
- **Utility split:** 2,715 utility-respondent cases and 7,243 non-utility-respondent cases.
- **Normalization:** I applied the rules in the order given (lowercase, strip non-alphanumerics, collapse spaces, then drop one trailing "inc/llc/co/corp/corporation/company"). For example, "Rieth-Riley Construction Co., Inc." becomes "riethriley construction co", and "Dyer Construction Co., Inc." becomes "dyer construction co".
- **Largest repeat respondents:** Miller Pipeline (91), Rieth-Riley (54), Calumet Civil Contractors (48), Milestone Contractors LP (46), and Milenium (36).

Caveats:
- Text extraction was messy. Order 110321 uses a layout where the respondent name sits in a separate block, so I parsed it with a separate rule. One respondent there wraps across two lines ("American Electric Power DBA Indiana Michigan / Power"), and I rejoined it. It counts as a utility either way.
- Two cases have a blank respondent. Their normalized name is empty, so they don't affect the 5-or-more count, but they are in the 7,243 denominator.
- Spelling variants such as "Internattional Inc." are kept as separate respondents, because the rule does not allow fuzzy matching.
- I did not OCR the original PDFs. The text layer appears complete, since the per-order page counts reconcile with the case count.
```

## Q7-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 98.7 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Expected cost per case: what is the mean headline penalty per case for non-utility-respondent cases in 2021, and in 2025? Include $0 cases. Round to the nearest dollar.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers**

- **2021: $2,370** per non-utility case (mean $2,370.49 over 1,230 cases)
- **2025: $3,041** per non-utility case (mean $3,040.75 over 1,227 cases)

**How I got them**

- I parsed the 58 text files in `data/text`, one recommendation form per page. The order year comes from the filename date, since the order text has no printed order date.
- Respondent was read from the form, including layouts where the labels print before the names. Utility status used the 13 listed prefixes.
- Headline penalty is the first `$` amount in the recommendation, with `$$` collapsed and spaces inside thousands handled. No `$` amount counts as $0, and zero-penalty cases are included.

**Judgment calls that affect the 2021 figure**

- Nine 2021 forms are in an older layout with a checkbox list. For eight of them (the 042121 order, pages 149–156), the first printed amount is "Training or $5,000", so I used $5,000. The text does not show which box is checked. If those are read as $0 instead, the 2021 mean falls by about $35.
- One 2021 form (Wickey Construction, 110321 page 168) has an OCR-garbled heading. Its recommendation is a warning letter, so I counted it as $0.
- One 2021 form (Pioneer/020321 page 27) prints ".5000" with no "$". Read as printed, it has no dollar amount, so I counted it as $0. If it is really $5,000, the 2021 mean rises by about $3.
- Several 2021 forms have a blank recommendation field and count as $0.
- The 2025 figure has no such ambiguities. Its only $0 utility-free zero besides warnings is one "Other" entry, which has no dollar amount in its text.
```

## Q7-r2

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 113.6 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Expected cost per case: what is the mean headline penalty per case for non-utility-respondent cases in 2021, and in 2025? Include $0 cases. Round to the nearest dollar.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers**

- **2021:** $2,378 per non-utility case (sum $2,917,200 over 1,227 cases, unrounded $2,377.51)
- **2025:** $3,041 per non-utility case (sum $3,731,000 over 1,227 cases, unrounded $3,040.75)

Both counts include the $0 cases: 503 in 2021 and 234 in 2025.

**Method**

- I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text`. The 2021 and 2025 scope covers 21 orders with 1,599 and 1,684 recommendation forms.
- Order dates come from the filename suffix (MMDDYY), because the order text does not print a date. Each file's order date is therefore inferred, not verified.
- I excluded 4 blank template pages (no case number) from 2021.
- Utility classification uses the respondent name against the 13 listed prefixes. The 2021 counts are 374 utility and 1,225 non-utility cases; 2025 has 457 utility and 1,227 non-utility.
- The headline penalty is the first printed "$" amount in the recommendation block, with "$$" and "$3, 500" normalised. A block with no "$" amount scores $0.

**Caveats that could move the numbers**

- **Checkbox form (2021):** eight non-utility forms in `ord_PSD_042121` are printed checkbox templates. Their first printed "$" is $5,000, which is what I used. If the marked box is the amount that counts, the figure changes (for example, the Pioneer Masonry form appears to mark $1,500 instead).
- **OCR-garbled penalty (2021):** one non-utility form in `ord_PSD_020321` (page 27) prints ".5000" with no "$". Under the rule as written it scores $0. If it is really $5,000, the 2021 mean rises by about $4 to roughly $2,382.
- **Respondent names:** I corrected a parsing error for label-separated forms in the 2021 order `ord_PSD_110321`. Respondent classification for the other forms follows their printed names.
```

## Q7-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 99.2 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Expected cost per case: what is the mean headline penalty per case for non-utility-respondent cases in 2021, and in 2025? Include $0 cases. Round to the nearest dollar.
```

```
**Expected cost per case (non-utility respondents, mean headline penalty, $0 cases included, rounded to nearest dollar):**

- **2021: $2,378** (n = 1,226 cases; total $2,915,700)
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Expected cost per case (non-utility respondents, mean headline penalty, $0 cases included, rounded to nearest dollar):**

- **2021: $2,378** (n = 1,226 cases; total $2,915,700)
- **2025: $3,041** (n = 1,227 cases; total $3,731,000)

**Method and caveats**

- **Order year:** The order text has no dates of its own, so I took each order's year from its filename (MMDDYY, or MM.DD.YY for `ord_PSD_09.04.24`). The 58 orders are all there.
- **Blank templates excluded:** Five pages are blank forms with no case number or respondent (four in 2021, one in 2022). I did not count them as cases. Including the four 2021 blanks as $0 cases gives $2,370.
- **Checkbox forms:** Eight 2021 forms (pages 149–156 of `ord_PSD_042121`) list penalty options as checkboxes. I applied the headline rule mechanically, so the first printed dollar amount is $5,000 on all eight. On page 156 (Pioneer Masonry) the checked box is $1,500, not $5,000. Using the checked amount instead gives $2,375 for 2021. The 2025 figure is unaffected.
- **Dollar parsing:** Recommendations with no "$" are $0, including ".5000" (order 020321, page 27), "Other", and "Training (completed …)". Amounts such as "$10,000; training or $5,000" take the first figure ($10,000).
```

## Q8-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 50.2 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Escalation: how many cases have a recommendation containing "ticket escalation" (any capitalization), and what is the most common headline penalty among those cases?
```

**System prompt:**

```
You are a careful analyst. You can use the Read, Grep, Glob and Bash tools (Bash has Python 3). Your working directory is the only place you may look for files; use only files in it. Show the number or answer each question asks for in your final reply.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 446 cases have a recommendation containing "ticket escalation." The most common headline penalty among them is $1,500, which appears in 291 of the 446 cases.**

Method and checks:
- I parsed all 58 order text files in `data/text` and found 9,964 case forms (one per "Case Number" page).
- The count matches the phrase inside the "recommends the following civil penalty" block, with line-wrapped matches included (for example, "ticket \nescalation"). Of the 446, 191 match on a single line and 255 are split across a line break.
- Every one of the 446 is the negated option "No ticket escalation - additional $1,000." The question's literal rule counts these, so I included them. If you meant only cases where escalation applies, the count is 0, since none of the 446 is a positive escalation.
- Headline penalty distribution among the 446: $1,500 (291), $3,000 (56), $5,000 (47), $3,500 (23), $4,000 (15), $4,500 (11), $1,000 (2), $6,000 (1). No case has a headline of $0.
- Ten checkbox-layout forms (mostly in `ord_PSD_042121`) also print the option "No ticket escalation." Six of them have it unchecked, and the other four lack it. I excluded them because the option is not a recommendation. Counting the unchecked labels would give 452 instead of 446, and $1,500 remains the mode either way.
```

## Q8-r2

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 19.5 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Escalation: how many cases have a recommendation containing "ticket escalation" (any capitalization), and what is the most common headline penalty among those cases?
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 452 cases** have a recommendation containing "ticket escalation." The most common headline penalty among them is **$1,500**, with 291 cases.

**How I got there**

- I parsed all 58 order text files in `/tmp/r2run-33ehzvyj/data/text/` and extracted the penalty block of each recommendation form.
- The match is case-insensitive and tolerates a line break inside the phrase. Several forms print "no ticket" and "escalation" on separate lines. A plain single-line grep finds only about 204 lines and misses these.
- I counted one case per form page. Some pages contain the phrase twice, so the raw match count is 459.

**Headline penalty among the 452 cases**

| Headline penalty | Cases |
|---|---|
| $1,500 | 291 |
| $3,000 | 56 |
| $5,000 | 53 |
| $3,500 | 23 |
| $4,000 | 15 |
| $4,500 | 11 |
| $1,000 | 2 |
| $6,000 | 1 |

**Caveats**

- Six of the 452 are from the 2021 checkbox-style forms (order PSD_042121, pages 149–156 of that file). In those, "No ticket escalation" is a printed option with an unchecked box, and the penalty options are not marked clearly enough in the text to say which was selected. Excluding them gives 446 cases. Their headline in the text is $5,000, so $1,500 remains the mode either way.
- The question's definition does not say whether "no ticket escalation" should count. Taken literally, as I did, it counts. Note that most matches are phrased "no ticket escalation - additional $1,000," which means the escalation does not apply.
```

## Q9-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 96.0 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Who gets hit: among non-utility-respondent cases, which "other party" name appears most often (exact text, ignoring case and surrounding spaces), and in what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases? Round to one decimal.
```
