**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: Northern Indiana Public Service Company, in 41.6% of non-utility-respondent cases (3,015 of 7,243).**

How I got there:

- **Data:** I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text/`. Each order has one or more recommendation forms, and I read the Respondent and Other Party fields from each.
- **Case count:** 9,957 cases after dropping 6 blank template pages with no respondent, other party, or case number. Of these, 2,714 have utility respondents and 7,243 are non-utility.
- **Tally:** Other-party names were compared with case and surrounding spaces ignored, after collapsing internal whitespace.

| Other party (non-utility respondents) | Cases | % of 7,243 |
|---|---|---|
| Northern Indiana Public Service Company | 3,015 | 41.6% |
| CenterPoint Energy Indiana | 2,490 | 34.4% |
| Citizens Gas | 923 | 12.7% |
| Vectren Energy Delivery | 201 | 2.8% |

**Caveats on the text layer:**

- Some fields are blank or garbled in the extracted text, so a few values are inferred from the surrounding lines. The original PDFs were not checked visually.
- Seven cases read "N orthem Indiana Public Service Company" (an OCR split). I did not merge them because the question asks for exact text. Merging them would give 3,022 cases, or 41.7%.
- 28 non-utility cases have no other-party name printed, so they count toward the denominator but not toward any name.
- One garbled form (order ord_PSD_020321, case number "0") was kept as a case. One respondent was blank (order ord_PSD_020823, case 28073) and was treated as non-utility. Neither changes the 41.6% figure.
```

## Q9-r2

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 29.0 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Who gets hit: among non-utility-respondent cases, which "other party" name appears most often (exact text, ignoring case and surrounding spaces), and in what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases? Round to one decimal.
```


**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: Northern Indiana Public Service Company is the most frequent other party among non-utility-respondent cases, at 41.6% (2,941 of 7,063).**

Method: I parsed all 58 order text files in `data/text/`, which contain 9,963 recommendation forms (cases). I classified each case's respondent with the utility prefix list, which left 7,063 non-utility-respondent cases (2,900 utility). I then counted the "Other Party" names in those 7,063 cases, ignoring case and surrounding spaces.

Top other parties among non-utility-respondent cases:

| Other party | Cases | % of 7,063 |
|---|---|---|
| Northern Indiana Public Service Company | 2,941 | 41.6% |
| CenterPoint Energy Indiana | 2,428 | 34.4% |
| Citizens Gas | 902 | 12.8% |

Caveats:
- One form (`ord_PSDNPT_040622.txt`, Case 24633) has its labels separated from their values, so the respondent and other party were read from the values that follow. I took both as CenterPoint Energy Indiana, which makes it a utility-respondent case. It does not change the top result.
- I did not de-duplicate case numbers. If any case appears in more than one order, the counts above would include it more than once.
```

## Q9-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 57.0 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Who gets hit: among non-utility-respondent cases, which "other party" name appears most often (exact text, ignoring case and surrounding spaces), and in what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases? Round to one decimal.
```

```
**Answer: Northern Indiana Public Service Company is the most common other party among non-utility-respondent cases: 3,012 of 7,243 cases, or 41.6%.**

Method:
- I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text` page by page. Each recommendation form is one case.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: Northern Indiana Public Service Company is the most common other party among non-utility-respondent cases: 3,012 of 7,243 cases, or 41.6%.**

Method:
- I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text` page by page. Each recommendation form is one case.
- I excluded 6 blank template pages that have no case number, leaving 9,958 cases.
- Utility respondents numbered 2,715 and non-utility respondents 7,243. The 7,243 is the denominator.
- Other-party names were compared after trimming, lowercasing, and collapsing internal whitespace. 28 non-utility cases have a blank other party and are counted in the denominator but not in any name.
- The runner-up is CenterPoint Energy Indiana at 2,489 (34.4%), followed by Citizens Gas at 922 (12.7%).

Caveats:
- Some pages have scrambled text-extraction layouts. I checked a sample of these and the respondent and other-party assignments looked right, but I did not check every page against the source PDFs.
- One non-utility case has a blank respondent. Treating it as utility would give 41.59%, which still rounds to 41.6%.
- "NIPSCO - Gas" appears as a separate exact string 14 times. I kept it separate because the question asks for exact text. Merging it would raise the share to about 41.8%.
```

```
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".

  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.

```
```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Expired tickets: among cases with base section 16(h), what percentage have the qualifier "expire", and what percentage have the qualifier "prior"? Round to one decimal.
```

```
You are a careful analyst. You can use the Read, Grep, Glob and Bash tools (Bash has Python 3). Your working directory is the only place you may look for files; use only files in it. Show the number or answer each question asks for in your final reply.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Expire: 12.3%** (483 of 3,939 cases with base section 16(h))
**Prior: 6.0%** (236 of 3,939)

Method: I extracted the IC 8-1-26 cite from every recommendation page in the 58 order texts, excluding each order's cover page. That gave 9,963 case pages. Cites with base 16(h) split into 3,021 with no qualifier, 483 "expire", 236 "prior", 180 "out" and 19 "other". Both percentages use all 3,939 base-16(h) cases as the denominator.

Caveats:
- Six case pages have no readable cite and are excluded. Five are blank forms; one (case 24409, ord_PSD_022322, page 129) has a case number but no cite text. I could not render the PDFs to check these, since pdftoppm is not installed.
- One case (ord_PSDNPT_040622, page 2) reads "IC 8-1-26-lS(h)". I treated it as not 16(h). Counting it as 16(h) gives 12.3% and 6.0% as well.
```

## H1-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 39.6 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Geography: define a case's **city** as the text after the last comma of its damage location, trimmed of spaces and lowercased, with spelling variants not merged; cases whose damage location has no comma or an empty city are left out of this question. Which city has the most cases, how many cases is that, and what percentage of the cases that have a city do the five most common cities together account for? Round the percentage to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: Indianapolis has the most cases, with 1,139. The five most common cities account for 26.1% of the cases that have a city.**

Top five cities (by last comma-separated part of the damage location, trimmed and lowercased):

| Rank | City | Cases |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | indianapolis | 1,139 |
| 2 | fort wayne | 524 |
| 3 | evansville | 443 |
| 4 | south bend | 293 |
| 5 | bloomington | 183 |

Top five total: 2,582 of 9,902 cases with a city, which is 26.08%, rounded to 26.1%.

Data notes:
- I parsed the 58 text extractions in `data/text/`, which contain 9,963 case blocks.
- In `ord_PSD_110321`, 141 forms had a blank "Damage Location" label in the text layer, but the address sat in the table text below it. I took the address from there (the line just before "The IURC…"). Using only the labeled values gives 2,547 of 9,763 cases, or 26.1% again, so the answer does not depend on this step.
- Nine blocks have no recoverable location in the text: eight forms in `ord_PSD_042121` (the text there lacks the location line) and one stray page-107 block in `ord_PSD_110321` with no content. I excluded them. Checking the PDFs would be needed to confirm them.
- 61 cases were left out for having no comma or an empty city, including the unrecoverable blocks.
- I did not verify the PDFs directly. The percentage is 26.1% under either treatment of the recovered locations.
```

## H1-r2

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 44.6 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Geography: define a case's **city** as the text after the last comma of its damage location, trimmed of spaces and lowercased, with spelling variants not merged; cases whose damage location has no comma or an empty city are left out of this question. Which city has the most cases, how many cases is that, and what percentage of the cases that have a city do the five most common cities together account for? Round the percentage to one decimal.
```

```
**Answer: Indianapolis, 1,153 cases. The five most common cities account for 26.2% of cases that have a city.**

- **Top city:** indianapolis, 1,153 cases.
- **Top five:** indianapolis 1,153; fort wayne 530; evansville 445; south bend 295; bloomington 184. Together 2,607.
- **Share:** 2,607 / 9,939 cases with a city = 26.2%.

Method and checks:
- I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text`. They contain 9,957 recommendation forms, one per "finds the following violation(s)" block, which matches the form count.
- 18 forms were left out of the city count: 7 had no usable damage location field, 9 had a blank city or no comma (e.g. "7401 ##", "3846 Schneider Street", "60687 County Road 37, Building Chicken,").
- The text extraction is imperfect for some pages where labels and values are split, so a few locations may be misread. I did not verify against the PDFs, since no PDF tool is installed here.
```

```
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: Indianapolis has the most cases, with 1,124. The five most common cities together account for 26.1% of cases that have a city.**

Method:
- I parsed the 58 order text files into recommendation forms. The text layer contains 9,964 case-number pages, of which 9,809 have a damage location line.
- The 9,809 includes cases with no comma (7) or an empty city after the last comma (35). Those are excluded, which leaves **9,763 cases with a city** and 590 distinct city strings.
- Spelling variants are not merged, per your definition.

Top five cities:

| Rank | City | Cases |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | indianapolis | 1,124 |
| 2 | fort wayne | 513 |
| 3 | evansville | 442 |
| 4 | south bend | 286 |
| 5 | bloomington | 182 |

Top five total: 2,547 of 9,763, which is 26.09%, rounded to **26.1%**.

Caveats:
- Eight case pages in `ord_PSD_042121` (pages 149–156) have no damage location in the text layer. Their form has no location line, so they are left out. I could not render the PDFs to confirm this because no PDF tools are installed here.
- The text-layer parse is the basis for these counts. I did not check every case against the PDF images.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers**

- **2023 mean reporting lag: 227 days** (227.34 unrounded, n = 2,107 cases)
- **2024 mean reporting lag: 188 days** (187.67 unrounded, n = 1,897 cases)
- **Share of all cases with lag > 365 days: 12.6%** (1,253 of 9,951 cases)

**Method and caveats**

- **Order date:** The order texts don't print an order date. I used the date encoded in each file name (MMDDYY, e.g. `ord_PSD_122821` = 12/28/2021) as the order date. The files are 58 orders, 2021 through 2025. If the file-name dates are not the order dates, these numbers will change.
- **Damage date:** Taken from "Date of Damage" on each recommendation form. Some forms use a table layout with no labels. I used the second date after the advisory-meeting date in those forms, and I added one form where the date sat before its label.
- **Excluded cases:** 6 forms have blank damage fields. 6 forms in `ord_PSD_042121` have two undated-label dates that match neither the advisory date, so I could not tell which one is the damage date. I excluded them. Their inclusion would change the results slightly.
- **Checks:** No lag was negative. Lags were computed for all 9,951 cases with a usable damage date, across all years.
```

## H2-r2

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Reporting lag: for each case with a parseable damage date (month/day/year), define the **lag** as the number of calendar days from the damage date to the order date. Using the order's year (not the damage year), what is the mean lag in days for cases in 2023 and in 2024, each rounded to the nearest whole day, and what percentage of all such cases (all years) have a lag of more than 365 days? Round the percentage to one decimal.
```

```
**Answers**

- **2023 mean lag: 227 days** (mean 227.34, n = 2,107 cases)
- **2024 mean lag: 188 days** (mean 187.67, n = 1,897 cases)
- **Share of all cases with lag > 365 days: 12.6%** (1,259 of 9,957 cases with a parseable damage date)

**How I got these**

- **Order date:** The order text does not print the order's date. I used the date in each filename (e.g., `ord_PSD_041024` → 2024-04-10, `ord_PSD_09.04.24` → 2024-09-04). As a check, no advisory-meeting date falls after its file's order date. This is an inference, not a printed date, so please confirm it against the PDFs.
- **Cases:** 58 orders contain 9,963 recommendation pages. Six are blank or unfilled forms with no damage date, so they are excluded. That leaves 9,957 cases with a parseable damage date.
- **Layout caveat:** Some OCR'd pages list values under their labels rather than beside them. For about 149 of those cases, I took the damage date as the second date on the page, after the advisory-meeting date. The lags look plausible: none are negative, and only two exceed three years.
- **Lag:** order date minus damage date, in calendar days. Year is the order's year.
```

## H2-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 56.2 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Reporting lag: for each case with a parseable damage date (month/day/year), define the **lag** as the number of calendar days from the damage date to the order date. Using the order's year (not the damage year), what is the mean lag in days for cases in 2023 and in 2024, each rounded to the nearest whole day, and what percentage of all such cases (all years) have a lag of more than 365 days? Round the percentage to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Results (2021-01-01 to 2025-12-31 orders, 9,951 cases with a parseable damage date):**

- **Mean lag, 2023 orders:** 227.34 days, so **227 days** (n = 2,107)
- **Mean lag, 2024 orders:** 187.67 days, so **188 days** (n = 1,897)
- **Share of all cases with lag > 365 days:** 1,253 of 9,951 = **12.6%**

**How I got these**

- The order text files in `data/text/` have no printed order date, so I took each order's date from its filename (for example, `ord_PSD_021225` is 2025-02-12; `ord_PSD_09.04.24` is 2024-09-04). I did not check this against the PDFs, which I could not read here.
- Damage dates come from the OCR text, where field labels and values are often separated. I matched each damage date to its case page and excluded the advisory-meeting date. No case had a damage date equal to the meeting date, and no lag was negative.

**Exclusions**

- 5 blank template pages (no case number or respondent) were dropped, leaving 9,952 cases.
- 1 case (case 24409, order 2022-02-23) has no damage date in the text, so it is not in any lag figure.
```

## H3-r1

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 30.0 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Abstentions: a case has a **recorded abstention** if its Abstentions field has any text left after trimming spaces and deleting any stray words "Additional Infonnation:" or "Additional Information:" that leaked into it. What percentage of cases in each of 2021, 2024 and 2025 (by order year) have a recorded abstention? Round each to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (order year, by filename date; case = recommendation form):**

- **2021: 41.3%** (662 of 1,603 cases have a recorded abstention)
- **2024: 54.0%** (1,025 of 1,897)
- **2025: 49.8%** (838 of 1,684)

**Method:**
- I parsed all 58 order texts in `data/text/`, split them by page, and kept each page with a "Case Number:" field as one case. That gives 9,963 cases across all years.
- For each case I took the text between "Abstentions:" and the next "Additional Information" (or "APPROVED"). I removed any "Additional Information:" or "Additional Infonnation:" leak, trimmed whitespace, and counted the case as having a recorded abstention if anything was left.
- Only two cases had a leaked label. One was "Smith Additional Infonnation:", which counts as recorded (the name "Smith" remains). The other was "Additional Infonnation:" alone, which does not count.

**Caveat:** The order texts don't print the order date, so I took each order's year from its filename (MMDDYY or MM.DD.YY). If the filenames don't match the order dates, the year groupings could shift.
```

```
- **2024: 54.0%** (1,025 of 1,897 cases)
- **2025: 49.8%** (838 of 1,684 cases)

**How I got them**

- The order text has no order date. I took each order's year from its filename (e.g., `ord_PSD_061924` → 2024, `ord_PSD_09.04.24` → 2024). The nunc pro tunc file `ord_PSDNPT_040622` is 2022 and doesn't affect these years. If the filename dates don't match the actual order dates, the year groupings could shift.
- For each "Abstentions:" field, I took the text up to the next "Additional Information" / "APPROVED" marker and trimmed whitespace. Any leaked "Additional Information:" text was removed. The only non-blank values left were commissioner names (Smith, Warren, Miller, Walters, Selking, Sontag, DeKemper, and a few combined forms), so no garbled text affected the count.
- Case totals are the number of "Abstentions:" fields per year, which matched the case-number count in each order.
```

## H3-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 67.9 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Abstentions: a case has a **recorded abstention** if its Abstentions field has any text left after trimming spaces and deleting any stray words "Additional Infonnation:" or "Additional Information:" that leaked into it. What percentage of cases in each of 2021, 2024 and 2025 (by order year) have a recorded abstention? Round each to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Abstention rates by order year (recorded abstention = Abstentions field has text after cleanup):**

| Year | Cases | Cases with recorded abstention | Percentage |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 1,603 | 662 | **41.3%** |
| 2024 | 1,897 | 1,025 | **54.0%** |
| 2025 | 1,684 | 838 | **49.8%** |

Method: I counted one case per "Abstentions:" label in the 58 order text files, which gives one case per recommendation form. I removed any stray "Additional Information:" text and trimmed spaces before testing for text. About 5,041 of the 9,965 cases have an empty field; the rest name a member, most often Smith or Warren.

Caveat: the order text does not print the order date, so I took each order's year from its filename date (e.g., `ord_PSD_011525` → 2025). I did not verify those dates against the PDFs.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Qualifier outcomes: a case has qualifier "expire", "prior" or "out" if its cite ends with that word in parentheses, on any base section. Among cases with each of those three qualifiers separately, what percentage have a headline penalty of $0? Round each to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (among cases with each qualifier, share with a headline penalty of $0):**

- **expire:** 184 of 517 cases = **35.6%**
- **prior:** 58 of 271 cases = **21.4%**
- **out:** 35 of 191 cases = **18.3%**

**How I got these:** I parsed all 58 order text files page by page. Each recommendation form was one case, and I read its cite and the first dollar amount in its penalty block. The counts match a separate raw count of the citation strings. Penalty blocks with no "$" (e.g., "Warning letter", "Training (completed)") were treated as $0. "$$1000" was read as $1,000 and "$3, 500" as $3,500.

**Caveats:**
- I excluded 6 pages with no readable cite: 5 are blank form templates with no case number, and one is case 24409 (Cobalt Civil, LLC, ord_PSD_022322), which has a penalty but no cite in the text. Case 24409 can't be assigned a qualifier, so it's outside all three groups.
- I could not render the original PDFs (pdftoppm is not installed), so I could not check those 6 pages or any OCR-damaged text against the originals.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Qualifier outcomes: a case has qualifier "expire", "prior" or "out" if its cite ends with that word in parentheses, on any base section. Among cases with each of those three qualifiers separately, what percentage have a headline penalty of $0? Round each to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (percentage of qualifier cases with a $0 headline penalty):**

- **expire:** 35.7% (185 of 518)
- **prior:** 21.7% (59 of 272)
- **out:** 18.3% (35 of 191)

These come from the 58 order texts in `data/text/`, which I parsed page by page. I found 981 qualifier cases in total.

**Method:**
- I treated each recommendation-form page as one case and read its cite from the "finds the following violation(s)" block. The qualifier is the parenthesized word at the end of that cite.
- The headline penalty is the first "$" amount in the recommendation block, with doubled "$" and spaces after thousands commas handled as you specified. A block with no "$" amount counts as $0.
- Every zero-penalty case in these groups is a warning letter, a training-only outcome, or a bare "0" with no dollar sign. No block with no "$" contained a dollar figure that the OCR might have garbled.

**Caveats:**
- The source text is OCR output with some errors, such as "fmds" for "finds" and "lS" for "15". I handled the variants that affect the qualifier and penalty fields. A residual OCR error could still shift a case by one, but I found none in the zero-penalty checks.
- One page (ord_PSD_022322, page 129, case 24409) has no readable cite in the text. It has no qualifier as parsed, so it does not affect these three numbers.
```

## H4-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 73.3 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Qualifier outcomes: a case has qualifier "expire", "prior" or "out" if its cite ends with that word in parentheses, on any base section. Among cases with each of those three qualifiers separately, what percentage have a headline penalty of $0? Round each to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (qualifier → share of cases with headline penalty $0):**

- **expire:** 184 of 517 cases = **35.6%**
- **prior:** 59 of 272 cases = **21.7%**
- **out:** 35 of 191 cases = **18.3%**

These are exact counts from the 58 order texts in `data/text`, not estimates.

**Method and caveats:**
- I counted each form page with a case as one case, per the definition. Blank template pages and cover pages were excluded.
- The headline penalty is the first printed dollar amount with digits in the recommendation. Checkbox-style forms (8 pages, all in `ord_PSD_042121`) print a $5,000 training line whether or not it is checked, so I read them as printed. Only one of those pages carries a qualifier, so this has little effect on the percentages.
- One form (`ord_PSD_022322`, p.129, Cobalt Civil) has no cite in the text. It has no qualifier, so it does not affect these counts.
```

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers**

**(a) Non-utility respondent cases with headline penalty ≥ $5,000**
- 2023: **41.5%** (623 of 1,502)
- 2024: **48.3%** (687 of 1,421)

**(b) Share of all cases that are utility respondent cases**
- 2023: **28.7%** (605 of 2,107)
- 2024: **25.1%** (476 of 1,897)

**Method**
- I parsed all 58 order text files in `data/text`, with one record per recommendation form. Every 2023 and 2024 form parsed completely (no missing respondent, cite, or recommendation).
- Year comes from the date in each order's filename. The filenames include one unusual format (`ord_PSD_09.04.24`), which I read as 2024.
- Headline penalty is the first dollar amount in the recommendation. Forms with no dollar amount (e.g., "Warning letter") count as $0.
- Utility status uses the respondent-name prefix list from the definitions, case-insensitive with leading spaces ignored.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers**

| Year | (a) Non-utility cases with headline penalty ≥ $5,000 | (b) Utility respondent share of all cases |
|---|---|---|
| 2023 | 623 of 1,502 = **41.5%** | 605 of 2,107 = **28.7%** |
| 2024 | 687 of 1,421 = **48.3%** | 476 of 1,897 = **25.1%** |

Between 2023 and 2024, the share of non-utility cases at or above $5,000 rose by about 6.8 points, and the utility share of all cases fell by about 3.6 points.

**Method and caveats**

- I parsed the 58 order text files in `data/text`, which gave 9,957 recommendation forms across all years. Each form is one case.
- The text files do not contain the order's signature date, so I took each order's date from its filename (for example, `ord_PSD_011724` → 2024-01-17, and `ord_PSD_09.04.24` → 2024-09-04). If the filenames do not match the order dates, the year assignments could shift.
- Headline penalty is the first dollar amount in the recommendation, with `$0` for cases that have no dollar amount (mostly "Warning letter" recommendations).
- Utility respondents were matched by the prefix list in the definitions, ignoring case and leading spaces. Naming variants such as "NIPSCO - Gas" and "Citizens Energy Group - Water" were counted as utilities.
- One 2023 case (case 28073, order `ord_PSD_020823`) has a blank respondent field in the source. I counted it as non-utility. Its penalty is $1,000, so it does not affect (a).
- The question does not use the IC 8-1-26 cite, so I did not use it. The cite text has some OCR errors (for example, "8-l-26"), which would matter for a question about base sections.
```

## H5-r3

arm `raw`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 88.9 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

2023 to 2024 shift: (a) among non-utility respondent cases, what percentage have a headline penalty of at least $5,000 in 2023 and in 2024; and (b) what percentage of all cases are utility respondent cases in 2023 and in 2024? Denominators include $0 cases. Round each percentage to one decimal.
```


**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**2023:** (a) 41.5% of non-utility cases have a headline penalty of at least $5,000 (623 of 1,502). (b) 28.7% of all cases are utility-respondent cases (605 of 2,107).

**2024:** (a) 48.3% of non-utility cases have a headline penalty of at least $5,000 (687 of 1,421). (b) 25.1% of all cases are utility-respondent cases (476 of 1,897).

Method and caveats:
- **Year:** The order text has no printed order date, so I took each order's year from its filename (MMDDYY, or MM.DD.YY for `ord_PSD_09.04.24`). This assumes the filename date is the order date. I did not verify it against the PDFs.
- **Coverage:** I parsed all 9,963 recommendation forms across the 58 orders. The 2023 and 2024 orders account for 25 files and 4,004 forms, and the form counts match the PDF page counts.
- **One unresolved respondent:** Case 28073 (order 020823, 2023) has a blank respondent field in the text. I treated it as non-utility, since its other party is Indiana Foundation Service, Inc. Its headline penalty is $1,000. Excluding it leaves the 2023 figure at 41.5%. If it were a utility, the 2023 utility share would be 28.8%, not 28.7%.
- **Utility rule:** I applied the prefix list exactly as defined. Some utility-like names are therefore non-utility, such as Fountaintown Gas Company, Indiana Natural Gas Corporation and Aurora Utilities.
- **Headline penalty:** This is the first dollar amount in each recommendation. Warning letters and "no penalty recommended" count as $0.
```
